2026-10-09T15:55:00Z | claude | fix | Adam: opening Connect 4 (and Aggravation, Monopoly) froze and crashed the site. Cause in skills/scene3d.js (every miniature): shaders compiled inside the first render, a blocking stall per board on first open and again when a new material first appears (the first disc); a repeat draw is ~10 ms. Long enough on some GPUs for a driver reset, and webglcontextlost was swallowed with no restore, so all 3D stayed dead. Fix: warmUp() compiles each scene (and its contact-shadow materials) with renderer.compileAsync (KHR_parallel_shader_compile in Chrome on a GPU) before it draws and after each update; webglcontextrestored recompiles and redraws every miniature. Checked headless (swiftshader has no parallel compile, so no speed-up shows there): connect4, monopoly, aggravation all draw, moves animate, no errors; figures/monopoly/connect4 tests pass.

Reads a .splat file on the device (no upload, no network), draws it with WebGL2 (sorted, alpha-blended Gaussians; the projection follows antimatter15/splat, MIT, which I fetched and read: 32-byte rows, covariance from the quaternion and scales) and a 2D canvas fallback. Proofs: the 2D covariance matches the covariance of 60 000 points sampled from the Gaussian and put through a real perspective projection (node); the GPU shader and the CPU path draw the same needles at six rotations (84% of lit pixels overlap, both looked at); the browser checks assert the demo draws, a non-splat says why, a real file loads, keys work, and reduced motion holds still (and its control, motion on, differs). Not done: .ply files (a clear message says convert to .splat), a URL field (a fetch from the page would need CORS and the page tests block outside), touch pinch tested only by reading, scans over 150 000 splats are thinned evenly. Owner call: a real scan to try (none in the repo).
